AI Engineering Roadmap

Build, evaluate, and operate grounded AI applications — 6 tiers, 19 prerequisite-based Levels.

Designed for Abdelrahman’s Node.js/Express backend experience, Python fundamentals, and SQL background. Based on the official ITI Native AI Developer syllabus, three selected AI Engineering books, and Anthropic’s Context Engineering article. Independent study guide; not affiliated with or endorsed by ITI.

Start here · Coverage matrix · Book chapters · Capstone · Progress · Sources

How the repository works

File Purpose
Each numbered Level/README.md Topics mapped directly to resources; prerequisites, outcomes, exercises, milestone, and completion gate.
Each numbered Level/resources.md Exact book sections, targeted official tutorials, practice drills, and reading-depth decisions.
curriculum.json Machine-readable Levels, dependencies, hours, ITI mapping, and exercises.
progress/tracker.csv Your status, evidence, and diagnostic waivers; generated dashboard in PROGRESS.md.
project/milestones Eight incremental acceptance contracts for one capstone.

Reading the roadmap

Label Meaning
ITI N01–N32 Local identifiers for exact titles from the published Native AI Developer brochure.
Industry extension Specific tools and operational depth added by this roadmap; not an official ITI requirement.
Difficulty 1–5 Relative learning complexity: introductory → advanced applied work; not seniority.
Personal hours Focused learning plus assigned implementation; not ITI classroom hours and not a guarantee.
P0 / P1 / P2 / P3 Essential now / first complete project / production quality / advanced or optional.

Topic-level learning resources

Every Level now maps individual subtopics to Arabic/Egyptian teaching or an explicit language gap, English lessons, verified book/article sections, official implementation references and a practical output. Its resource library separates videos, runnable material, paid alternatives, practice, documentation and optional depth.

Resource policy · All-Level coverage audit · Reference comparison

Choose one explanation per gap and build. The expanded library contains alternatives; it does not increase the required path to every listed course.

Your starting advantage

Already practiced Review quickly Learn deliberately
Express REST, middleware, validation, JWT/RBAC, Jest/Supertest Pass L00 diagnostic and map concepts to Python Typing versus runtime validation; Pydantic, dependency lifecycle, pytest fixtures.
Python syntax/OOP and SQL concepts Skip a full beginner course after diagnostic evidence Async I/O, SQLAlchemy sessions, migrations, cancellation, ownership-aware retrieval.
Git and Docker basics Demonstrate a clean setup and container build Evaluation gates, prompt/data versions, traces, secrets, rollback.

The complete roadmap

Follow prerequisites, not a weekly calendar. Build from L01; do not wait to finish all reading. Foundations L02/L03 are bounded labs, not a Data Science specialization.

Tier 1 · Engineering and Model Foundations

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L00 · Baseline Assessment and Product Scope N01, N03, N09, N30, N31 Identify transferable backend skills and specific gaps. Define users, requirements, failure cases, and acceptance evidence. 1/5 Diagnostic 3–5 h
L01 · Python, FastAPI, and Service Testing N02, N05, N07 Use type hints, Pydantic, dependency injection, and lifespan management. Separate HTTP handlers from application logic and test the service. 2/5 L00 14–20 h
L02 · Practical Mathematics, ML, and Data Preparation N11, N12, N13 Understand vectors, matrices, similarity, probability, loss, and gradients through small examples. Run an ML baseline with correct splits, preprocessing, and evaluation. Recognize leakage and distinguish a similarity score from a calibrated probability. 2/5 L00 16–24 h
L03 · Deep Learning, Foundation Models, and Serving N14, N15, N16 Distinguish pretraining, supervised fine-tuning, preference tuning, and inference. Understand layers, autograd, attention, tokenization, sampling, and resource constraints. Choose an external provider or local inference endpoint with a reasoned boundary. 3/5 L00 12–18 h

Tier 2 · Reliable LLM Application Basics

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L04 · Prompt Engineering, Structured Outputs, and LLM Integration N04, N16 Design instructions, examples, schemas, and untrusted-input boundaries. Integrate one model behind an adapter with controlled error handling. 2/5 L01, L03 10–16 h
L05 · Evaluation Engineering N05, N20 Define datasets and rubrics before expanding the application. Distinguish deterministic tests, component evaluation, and end-to-end quality. 3/5 L04 14–22 h
L06 · PostgreSQL, ORM, and AI Application State N06, N07, N09 Move from Mongoose-style ODM experience to SQLAlchemy relations, sessions, transactions, and Alembic. Design document, conversation, and journal ownership and versions. 2/5 L01 12–18 h

Tier 3 · Grounded Retrieval and Context

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L07 · RAG Document Preparation, Embeddings, and Semantic Search N12, N17, N19 Convert a small textual PDF into traceable chunks with page provenance. Compare a lexical baseline with embeddings appropriate to the application language. 3/5 L03, L06 16–24 h
L08 · RAG Systems, Citations, and Retrieval Improvement N17, N19, N20 Build retrieval → context → generation with verifiable citations. Diagnose failures and justify hybrid search or reranking through comparison. 3/5 L05, L07 18–28 h
L09 · Context Engineering, Memory, and Conversations N04, N19, N22 Budget instructions, evidence, selected journals, history, and memory. Manage memory provenance, correction, deletion, and summarization loss. 3/5 L08 14–22 h

Tier 4 · Production Behavior and Tool Use

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L10 · Concurrency, Streaming, and Long-Running Jobs N02, N18, N25 Separate I/O concurrency from CPU parallelism and avoid blocking the event loop. Handle streaming cancellation, timeouts, and restartable ingestion jobs. 3/5 L08 16–24 h
L11 · Security, Authorization, and Responsible AI N07, N20, N26 Enforce ownership before retrieval, source viewing, history, memory, and job access. Constrain untrusted content, tools, uploads, and consumption. 3/5 L06, L08 16–24 h
L12 · Tool Calling, Bounded Agents, and Multi-Agent Decisions N20, N22, N24 Distinguish fixed workflows from model-directed tool loops. Implement safe tool contracts and evaluate tool/task failures. Understand sub-agent patterns without assuming they improve the product. 4/5 L05, L09, L11 18–28 h
L13 · MCP, Enterprise Integration, and AI Workflows N23, N24 Understand MCP host/client/server roles, primitives, transport, and authorization. Build one bounded integration with explicit failure behavior. 3/5 L12 10–16 h

Tier 5 · Operating and Demonstrating AI Systems

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L14 · MLOps, LLMOps, Architecture, and Deployment N01, N05, N09, N10, N15, N25 Version model/prompt/corpus/evaluation artifacts and trace execution. Release, restore, and diagnose an AI service and its worker. 4/5 L10, L11 20–30 h
L15 · Model Selection, Cost, Latency, and User Feedback N20, N25 Improve a measured bottleneck without sacrificing quality or isolation. Interpret user feedback as a signal rather than unquestioned labels. 4/5 L05, L14 12–18 h
L16 · Capstone Release, Technical Defense, and Career Evidence N27, N28, N29, N30, N31, N32 Demonstrate a usable AI application with evaluation and operating evidence. Explain and modify the implementation independently and communicate honest limitations. 4/5 L02, L03, L08, L09, L10, L11, L12, L13, L14, L15 12–18 h

Tier 6 · Optional Specialization and Supporting UI

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L17 · Fine-Tuning, Dataset Engineering, and Advanced Serving N12, N15, N21 Choose between prompt changes, RAG, model selection, and fine-tuning using failure evidence. Understand SFT/LoRA, curation, quantization, and serving tradeoffs. 4/5 L02, L03, L05, L15 18–32 h
L18 · Supporting TypeScript and React UI N05, N08 Build a small accessible client for API errors, streaming, and evidence display. Distinguish product UI needs from a complete React/TypeScript curriculum. 2/5 L01, L04, L11 12–20 h

Learning progression

Exact Mermaid diagrams are available in the repository Markdown; prerequisite tables remain readable here.

This compact graph shows the main branches; the Level tables and manifest are the full prerequisite contract. L12 additionally depends on L05; L16 consolidates all core gates.

Pick a sustainable pace

Core path: 233–355 focused hours. Level estimates already include their assigned capstone work; do not add milestone hours again. Optional Levels are separate. Allow contingency for debugging and prior gaps.

Hours/week Core estimate before contingency How to adapt
8–10 23–44 weeks Finish one small gate at a time; keep the corpus and provider count small.
15–20 12–24 weeks Default pace: about 60% implementation, 25% reading, 15% evaluation and notes.
25–30 8–14 weeks Use extra time for tests and failure analysis before adding frameworks.

One capstone, built incrementally

Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant: a FastAPI service that retrieves permitted source passages, cites evidence, connects user-selected journal entries, and manages bounded context. It is a reflection/productivity tool; outputs distinguish source claims from suggestions.

Stage Deliverable Level checkpoint
S0 Scope and Baseline L00
S1 Testable Python API L00, L01
S2 LLM Integration and Scientific Foundations L01, L02, L03, L04
S3 Measured RAG MVP L05, L06, L07, L08
S4 Safe Context-Aware Product L09, L10, L11
S5 Tools, Agents, and MCP L12, L13
S6 Operations and Optimization L14, L15
S7 Portfolio Release and Technical Defense L16

Progress and completion

python scripts/progress.py next
python scripts/progress.py set L00 in_progress
python scripts/progress.py set L00 completed --evidence "evidence/baseline.md"
python scripts/validate.py
python -m unittest discover -s tests -v

Progress instructions explain evidence and waiver requirements. Reading a chapter is not completion: show working behavior, relevant tests, and a decision you can explain without generated code.

Scope decisions

Essential Add only when justified Defer
FastAPI, one model adapter, PostgreSQL/pgvector, evaluation fixtures Hybrid search, reranking, persistent jobs, bounded tools, MCP Multi-agent systems, fine-tuning, GPU optimization, orchestration frameworks.
ML/DL concepts through small CPU labs Extra architecture depth after measured failures Research-level derivations, large training runs, Data Engineering specialization.

Sources, coverage, and limitations

The coverage matrix maps every published syllabus row to Levels and evidence. Representation is not mastery. Optional frontend and fine-tuning material remains visible because ITI names it, while the core path prioritizes practical AI systems. The brochure advertises 678 hours while its listed rows sum to 696; that discrepancy is retained without inventing a correction.

The supplied book contents and relevant sections were checked in the existing research. Sources and pagination explain exact editions, verification boundaries, and resource links. Current provider prices and interview questions are not asserted.

Repository structure and quality

Repository guide · Evaluation · Security · Context Engineering · Career evidence · Learning with AI

Organizational inspiration: Data Engineering Roadmap 2026. Its tier/Level navigation and topic-to-resource approach informed this rebuild; its subjects, hours, level count, and text were not copied.

Start with L00, then build the first tested endpoint in L01.

Home · Roadmap · Progress

Your first 2–3-hour session

Time Task Evidence
20 min Read the README and run python scripts/progress.py next Identify L00 as the entry point
30 min Complete the L00 diagnostic Three specific gaps, not a new beginner curriculum
30 min Read C1 Planning AI Applications, printed pp. 28–34 A short product scope
60–90 min Start L01: environment, FastAPI, /health, and one HTTP test A working endpoint in a separate application repository
10 min Save evidence and update progress honestly One recorded next action

Working rules

Rule Apply it this way
Follow prerequisites Level numbers are organizational; the manifest defines dependencies
Build early L00 → L01 + L03 → L04 → L05; then L06/L07 → L08
Keep foundations Complete L02 alongside implementation; it remains required for Core
Avoid redundant reading G for intuition, F for implementation, C for evaluation and decisions
Earn shortcuts Waive a Level only after passing all its gates and recording evidence
Use one project Keep application code in a separate repository; do not rewrite CRM for this roadmap
Control model calls Fakes for deterministic tests; budgeted live calls for model evaluation

Suggested first-week evidence: scope, a typed API, fake provider, tests, setup instructions, and 10–15 authored evaluation cases. Do not wait to finish entire books before coding.

ITI Native AI Developer Coverage

Home · Sources · Reading

The official 2026/2027 brochure is five pages. Page 2 publishes prerequisites and678 hours/about five months; pages4–5 list the items below, totaling696 hours. The discrepancy is unresolved. N identifiers are local traceability IDs, not official module numbers.

Official prerequisites: basic/intermediate programming, preferably Python; data structures/algorithms/problem solving; SQL and relational concepts; Git/command line; conceptual statistics/linear algebra and technical English.

Coverage limits and deliberate choices

Topic Treatment
SQL/NoSQL New ORM/session/migration work; existing MongoDB knowledge is assessed rather than repeated
Frontend L18 explicitly covers supporting React/TypeScript; Core needs a basic UI but does not claim the full42-hour React syllabus
Math/ML/DL L02/L03 retain small scientific labs; the three books alone are not a full ML curriculum
Fine-tuning L17 remains visible as optional advanced study; no practical training claim without an actual run
Multi-agent Understand patterns and evaluate a design; implementation is conditional on measured value
MCP L13 includes a small server/client integration using current official documentation
Governance/compliance Threat modeling, privacy boundaries, retention, and responsible behavior; no comprehensive legal/compliance certification claimed
Employability Demonstrations, technical communication, evidence-based CV claims, ethics, and scoped delivery exercises

The mapping is complete traceability, not a claim that self-study equals official training depth or accreditation. Study hours and dependency choices are recommendations.

Industry extensions inside ITI-backed Levels

Recommendation Levels Source boundary
pytest, Pydantic, SQLAlchemy, Alembic L01/L06 Chosen implementations of Python, testing, backend, database topics; not claimed as named ITI mandates.
pgvector, hybrid retrieval, reranking L07/L08 Tool choice and depth added to embeddings/RAG topic coverage.
Durable job semantics, cancellation, CI gates L10/L14 Operational implementation detail recommended beyond the brochure.
OWASP attack fixtures, owner-filtered retrieval, memory deletion L09/L11 Concrete tests and security design supplied by this roadmap.
OpenTelemetry, p95 latency, cost budgets L14/L15 Recommended instrumentation and measurable acceptance evidence.

Chapter and Section Reading Guide

Home · Levels · Sources

Code Book Page convention
G Grokking AI Applications — Andrea De Mauro Printed page numbers
F Building Generative AI Services with FastAPI — Alireza Parandeh PDF viewer pages, starting at1
C AI Engineering — Chip Huyen Printed page numbers

The tables of contents and relevant excerpts were checked in the supplied PDFs. These are locators, not assignments to read every page. The module file defines the exact pass and prerequisites. Read a concept once, implement it in the same application, and consult the second source for a specific unresolved question.

Chapter Verified location Depth and focus Module

Additional area Why another source is necessary
Practical ML/DL L02/L03 use focused scikit-learn/PyTorch documentation; the three books do not provide a complete foundations course
MCP L13 requires official protocol/SDK documentation

Deep reading means implementing and explaining a decision, not reproducing every example. Low-code setup, duplicate chatbots, and unneeded model modalities are intentionally minimized.

Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant

Home · Architecture · Evaluation

Implement the application yourself in a separate repository. Link its commits, tests, reports, and demos as evidence here. This package is a learning repository, not a finished product.

Milestone Required modules Delivery
S0 L00 Scope and Baseline
S1 L00, L01 Testable Python API
S2 L01, L02, L03, L04 LLM Integration and Scientific Foundations
S3 L05, L06, L07, L08 Measured RAG MVP
S4 L09, L10, L11 Safe Context-Aware Product
S5 L12, L13 Tools, Agents, and MCP
S6 L14, L15 Operations and Optimization
S7 L16 Portfolio Release and Technical Defense
Product behavior Acceptance direction
Upload Start with a small permitted text PDF; record parsing failures and provenance
Ask Answer from authorized relevant passages and show verifiable citations
Reflect Connect a user-selected journal entry with source ideas; distinguish interpretation from source claims
Continue Maintain bounded history and editable, sourced memory
Act Use bounded authorized tools only when justified
Operate Evaluate, trace, recover, and report operating limits

S3 is the MVP; S4 is intermediate; S5–S7 add integration and production-oriented evidence. S5 and S6 may proceed independently after prerequisites. L17 and broader multi-agent implementations are optional. The capstone requires a basic UI; React specifically is the L18 option, not a Core blocker.

Initial Architecture Decisions

Project · ADR template

Component Initial choice Reason
API FastAPI and Pydantic Transfer backend knowledge with typed contracts
State PostgreSQL, SQLAlchemy, Alembic Relations, ownership, transactions, and migrations
Retrieval pgvector One database to start; exact retrieval before measured ANN need
Models One direct-SDK provider adapter Keep integration inspectable and testable
Files Private local storage, then persistent volume/object storage Preserve source access and deployment state
Jobs One worker/queue when necessary Isolate long-running work and test retry semantics
Architecture Modular monolith Avoid premature service boundaries

Exact Mermaid diagrams are available in the repository Markdown; prerequisite tables remain readable here.

Contract Required behavior
Authorization Scope retrieval and source access before content enters context
Citations Model references come from a whitelist; the server validates IDs and displays source metadata
Ownership The model cannot choose an authoritative user_id or execute arbitrary SQL
Provenance Save document/page/offset/hash/chunker/model/dimension/version/status
Sessions API and concurrent worker tasks do not share one mutable session
Memory Preserve source/date/owner and support edit/delete
Search Exact nearest-neighbor search is not a guarantee of relevant evidence; model dimensions are not guessed from generation-model hidden size

Initial entities: users, documents, document_versions, chunks, ingestion_runs, conversations, messages, and journal_entries. Record actual alternatives in ADRs rather than treating these recommendations as official ITI tools.

L00 · Baseline Assessment and Product Scope

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Tier Focused effort Difficulty Priority Required before starting
1 · Engineering and Model Foundations 3–5 h 1/5 P0 Existing backend/Python/SQL experience

Unlocks: L01, L02, L03

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Identify transferable backend skills and specific gaps. Define users, requirements, failure cases, and acceptance evidence. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
Backend transfer Explain validation, auth, persistence and one integration test from CRM.
Python/SQL baseline Write a JSON transformation and a small join without generated code.
Product reasoning Name a user, their question, a failure and observable success.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

C1 planning → mini-RAG orientation → your scope and diagnostic. Spend hours defining a testable first slice, not choosing a framework.

Pass Work to do Stop condition
1 · Orient Use the assigned book/article focus and one Arabic or English explanation for each unfamiliar topic. Explain the mechanism in your own words.
2 · Implement Work through the numbered topic outputs below; integrate them into the assessed exercises. The specified behavior is reproducible in your own code.
3 · Verify Run the failure cases, self-check and milestone acceptance contract. Evidence and remaining limitations are recorded.

The resource library contains alternatives and optional depth. The effort estimate covers a selected path plus implementation, not watching every listed course. Choose Arabic teaching when it speeds understanding, then consult the English API reference for exact behavior.

What you learn and exactly where

Each numbered row is a learning unit. Arabic teaching, machine-translated Arabic text, English videos, and English documentation are labeled separately. A missing verified Arabic resource is stated explicitly; a loosely related video is not presented as full coverage.

Topic and required subtopics Arabic route English route Build or verify
L00-T01 · Transfer backend skills
Express handlers → FastAPI routes; Zod → Pydantic; Jest → pytest; Mongoose → ORM boundaries		 Pythonation FastAPI tutorial and companion code (AR video + code)

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L00-E1 Explain a JWT/RBAC flow, Zod validation, and an integration test from CRM. Write a typed JSON cleaner and a test without AI. Record demonstrated skills and gaps; distinguish prior evidence from assumptions.
L00-E2 Write five user stories and three failure cases: missing evidence, unreadable document, and unauthorized resource access. Each story has an observable acceptance condition.
L00-E3 Choose a small permitted corpus, specify input/output contracts and three non-goals, and create the first ADR. A one-page scope and ten initial questions are sufficient to start building.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Planning everything Freeze three non-goals and build the first endpoint next.
Vague AI success Turn “helpful answer” into groundedness, citation and abstention criteria.
Copying a tutorial product Use your own reflection/journal user stories and corpus.

8 · Optional advanced reading

No separate advanced reading is required. Use the Level postpone list and complete the practical gate first.

How to choose and stop

Situation Action
Already comfortable from backend work Demonstrate the diagnostic, skim repeated theory and build the Python-specific behavior.
Arabic introduction helps Use it first, then verify English API names and error behavior in the official reference.
Two sources cover the same thing Finish one explanation, attempt the exercise, and use the second only for the unresolved part.
Tutorial differs from your version Check current docs, pin dependencies and record the adjustment in an ADR.
Everything in the completion gate passes Record the evidence and move to an unlocked Level; bonus reading stays optional.

Verification: resource pages and author/publisher indexes were checked on 2026-10-16. Reachability, title/scope evidence and verification limits are recorded in the audit; the inspectable catalog stores each topic assignment. No claim is made that every video was watched end-to-end or that paid enrollment was tested.

L01 · Python, FastAPI, and Service Testing

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Tier Focused effort Difficulty Priority Required before starting
1 · Engineering and Model Foundations 14–20 h 2/5 P0 L00

Unlocks: L04, L06, L18

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Use type hints, Pydantic, dependency injection, and lifespan management. Separate HTTP handlers from application logic and test the service. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
Python transfer Use functions, OOP, exceptions, modules and virtual environments without a beginner walkthrough.
HTTP transfer Explain status codes, validation and middleware from Express.
Testing transfer Write one success and one failure assertion independently.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

F2/F4 selective reading → mini-RAG lessons 5–6 or CS50 testing → a fake-provider API. Add pytest from the first endpoint.

Pass Work to do Stop condition
1 · Orient Use the assigned book/article focus and one Arabic or English explanation for each unfamiliar topic. Explain the mechanism in your own words.
2 · Implement Work through the numbered topic outputs below; integrate them into the assessed exercises. The specified behavior is reproducible in your own code.
3 · Verify Run the failure cases, self-check and milestone acceptance contract. Evidence and remaining limitations are recorded.

The resource library contains alternatives and optional depth. The effort estimate covers a selected path plus implementation, not watching every listed course. Choose Arabic teaching when it speeds understanding, then consult the English API reference for exact behavior.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L01-E1 Implement /health and POST /reflect with an explicitly fake provider. Test valid, missing, invalid, and provider-timeout cases. Tests make no live model calls and assert documented responses.
L01-E2 Write a context manager for a resource and a generator for batched records. An exception triggers cleanup; batches preserve the expected records.
L01-E3 Replace the provider using a dependency override. The API contract stays the same when fake and real adapters are swapped.
L01-E4 Explain annotations versus runtime validation and def versus async def. Add one endpoint without copying a solution. Demonstrate the distinction with a failing validation case; deepen concurrency in L10.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Annotations treated as validation Show a plain typed function accepting a bad value and a Pydantic model rejecting it.
One global mutable client/session Initialize shared clients in lifespan; use request/task-specific state.
Tests call a paid model Override the provider and make tests deterministic.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. Why Depends rather than manual construction in every route?
  2. Does async make CPU work faster?
  3. What must cleanup prove?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 It provides a declared lifecycle and an override seam while keeping application logic testable.
2 No; it enables cooperative I/O concurrency. CPU work needs a different execution boundary.
3 Resources close on both success and exceptions; tests verify the teardown.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S1 Application skeleton in a separate implementation repository; Tests, pinned dependencies, and setup instructions A clean checkout runs the API and tests. Provider failures have defined responses, and you can add and test a small feature independently.

Completion criteria

A clean checkout runs the API and tests. Provider failures have defined responses, and you can add and test a small feature independently.

Postpone until justified

  • A complete Python beginner course if diagnostics pass
  • Agent frameworks and elaborate class hierarchies

python scripts/progress.py set L01 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

L02 · Practical Mathematics, ML, and Data Preparation

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Tier Focused effort Difficulty Priority Required before starting
1 · Engineering and Model Foundations 16–24 h 2/5 P1 L00

Unlocks: L16, L17

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Understand vectors, matrices, similarity, probability, loss, and gradients through small examples. Run an ML baseline with correct splits, preprocessing, and evaluation. Recognize leakage and distinguish a similarity score from a calibrated probability. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
Math baseline Manipulate arrays and explain a mean, probability and function slope.
Python prerequisite Write functions and load a small tabular dataset.
Application link Explain why retrieval similarity and predictive confidence are different.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

Selected Arabic regression/classification or visual math → scikit-learn split/pipeline lab. Keep the scope to foundations that explain AI-system decisions.

Pass Work to do Stop condition
1 · Orient Use the assigned book/article focus and one Arabic or English explanation for each unfamiliar topic. Explain the mechanism in your own words.
2 · Implement Work through the numbered topic outputs below; integrate them into the assessed exercises. The specified behavior is reproducible in your own code.
3 · Verify Run the failure cases, self-check and milestone acceptance contract. Evidence and remaining limitations are recorded.

The resource library contains alternatives and optional depth. The effort estimate covers a selected path plus implementation, not watching every listed course. Choose Arabic teaching when it speeds understanding, then consult the English API reference for exact behavior.

What you learn and exactly where

Each numbered row is a learning unit. Arabic teaching, machine-translated Arabic text, English videos, and English documentation are labeled separately. A missing verified Arabic resource is stated explicitly; a loosely related video is not presented as full coverage.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L02-E1 Calculate dot products, norms, and cosine for [1,0] and [0,1]; implement zero-vector handling and explain matrix shapes. Check orthogonal-vector similarity, identical-vector similarity, and the zero-vector policy.
L02-E2 Build a confusion matrix from eight predictions and calculate precision, recall, and F1. Show denominators and handling of undefined cases; explain what each metric misses.
L02-E3 Train a small logistic regression or decision tree on public toy or synthetic data; compare a majority baseline. Fit preprocessing only on training data; save split, seed, and metrics.
L02-E4 Create an intentionally leaky experiment, then repair it. Identify the leaked information and reproduce both configurations; no target score is required.
L02-E5 Explain a small loss curve, gradient descent, overfitting, and regularization. Describe the direction of an update and the difference between training and generalization.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Leakage during preprocessing Fit transforms only on training data and prove it in a pipeline.
Accuracy on an imbalanced set Compare to a trivial baseline and inspect precision/recall.
Turning this into a long DS track Stop after one correct baseline and a short explanation of uncertainty.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. What is data leakage?
  2. Why normalize vectors?
  3. Is cosine 0.8 an 80% probability?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 Training or selection uses information unavailable at prediction time, including test-set preprocessing.
2 It changes similarity geometry; cosine compares direction after accounting for magnitude.
3 No. It is a geometric score, not a calibrated event probability.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S2 math-notes.md and a small reproducible ML script or notebook; Leakage comparison with data source or generation seed Compute simple similarity and classification metrics, explain train/validation/test, and reproduce the ML experiment without leaking test information.

Completion criteria

Compute simple similarity and classification metrics, explain train/validation/test, and reproduce the ML experiment without leaking test information.

Postpone until justified

  • A full Data Science specialization
  • Advanced proofs or prolonged competition tuning

Record completion

Run from the roadmap root:

python scripts/progress.py set L02 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

The tracker checks dependency/evidence fields; it cannot assess engineering skill automatically.

L03 · Deep Learning, Foundation Models, and Serving

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Tier Focused effort Difficulty Priority Required before starting
1 · Engineering and Model Foundations 12–18 h 3/5 P0 L00

Unlocks: L04, L07, L16, L17

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Distinguish pretraining, supervised fine-tuning, preference tuning, and inference. Understand layers, autograd, attention, tokenization, sampling, and resource constraints. Choose an external provider or local inference endpoint with a reasoned boundary. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
Math readiness (review L02 if needed) Explain a loss, gradient, train/test split and vector shape.
Python readiness Use a small tensor/array and inspect dimensions.
System boundary Separate a web server process from a model-serving process.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

G1 + C2 conceptual sections → visual attention/backpropagation → a tiny CPU PyTorch lab and one inference endpoint. Train no large model here.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L03-E1 Implement a tiny linear layer, loss, backward pass, and optimizer step in PyTorch. Record tensor shapes, inspect gradients, and explain the update. Use L02 exercise E1 first if vectors are unfamiliar.
L03-E2 Explore tokenization for representative inputs, optionally including Arabic and English. Distinguish characters, tokens, context capacity, and embedding dimensions.
L03-E3 Run five prompts using two sampling settings on one model. Record settings and variation; low temperature is not treated as a determinism guarantee.
L03-E4 Draw client → FastAPI → provider and compare external versus local serving. Document timeouts, lifecycle, privacy/resource constraints, and actual hardware assumptions.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Skipping shapes Print shape/dtype/device at each step and assert expected dimensions.
Assuming sampling is fully deterministic Record model/version/config and repeat outputs; temperature zero is not a universal reproducibility guarantee.
Training before integration Use a CPU toy model for understanding; integrate an existing model for the app.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. What does backpropagation compute?
  2. Why do token counts differ in Arabic and English?
  3. What is post-training versus inference?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 Derivatives of a loss with respect to parameters using the chain rule.
2 The tokenizer vocabulary and segmentation differ; measure the actual model tokenizer.
3 Post-training updates parameters or preferences; inference uses the selected parameters to produce outputs.

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S2 tiny-autograd.py and tokenization observations; Model selection card and serving ADR Explain next-token prediction and attention conceptually, demonstrate the autograd lab, and run inference. L02 may proceed in parallel; completing all ML topics is not a prerequisite for the first model API call.

Completion criteria

Explain next-token prediction and attention conceptually, demonstrate the autograd lab, and run inference. L02 may proceed in parallel; completing all ML topics is not a prerequisite for the first model API call.

Postpone until justified

  • Training a transformer from scratch
  • Audio/video/3D serving and distributed GPU training

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Design instructions, examples, schemas, and untrusted-input boundaries. Integrate one model behind an adapter with controlled error handling. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
L01 readiness A fake provider can be replaced through a tested dependency.
Model readiness Explain tokens, sampling and context limits.
Contract readiness Specify expected response fields and provider error behavior.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

G2 code section + G3 → one provider adapter → validated output → a fixed prompt comparison. Read C5 for failure analysis after the first call.

Pass Work to do Stop condition
1 · Orient Use the assigned book/article focus and one Arabic or English explanation for each unfamiliar topic. Explain the mechanism in your own words.
2 · Implement Work through the numbered topic outputs below; integrate them into the assessed exercises. The specified behavior is reproducible in your own code.
3 · Verify Run the failure cases, self-check and milestone acceptance contract. Evidence and remaining limitations are recorded.

The resource library contains alternatives and optional depth. The effort estimate covers a selected path plus implementation, not watching every listed course. Choose Arabic teaching when it speeds understanding, then consult the English API reference for exact behavior.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L04-E1 Use the supplied synthetic excerpts to return an answer and references drawn from a supplied ID list. Validate the response schema on the server and reject unknown reference IDs.
L04-E2 Test valid JSON containing a wrong claim, malformed output, timeout, and HTTP429. Separate schema failure, factual failure, and transport failure.
L04-E3 Compare zero-shot with two few-shot examples on ten cases. Save prompt/model versions, usage, latency, and the observed differences.
L04-E4 Implement bounded retries for selected transient failures. Permanent failures do not loop; time and attempt limits are tested.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Prompt only works for the demo Use fixed ordinary, ambiguous and adversarial cases.
Schema equals truth Validate output structure and separately evaluate grounding/correctness.
Blind retry loops Retry only transient failures within a total deadline and record usage.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. What is prompt versioning?
  2. Why an adapter?
  3. Can JSON mode guarantee a correct answer?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 Store template, examples, model/config and test results so comparisons are reproducible.
2 Provider SDK and transport details stay outside business logic and test contracts.
3 No; parseability, schema validity and factual correctness are different checks.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S2 Provider adapter, versioned prompt, and structured-output tests; Small prompt comparison report No secrets are committed. Valid JSON is not mistaken for a correct answer. The same application logic works with fake and real providers.

Completion criteria

No secrets are committed. Valid JSON is not mistaken for a correct answer. The same application logic works with fake and real providers.

Postpone until justified

  • Many providers at once
  • Prompt frameworks or autonomous agent loops

Record completion

python scripts/progress.py set L04 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

The tracker checks dependency/evidence fields; it cannot assess engineering skill automatically.

L05 · Evaluation Engineering

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Define datasets and rubrics before expanding the application. Distinguish deterministic tests, component evaluation, and end-to-end quality. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
Working endpoint Run the same request repeatedly and capture output/config.
Test separation Distinguish HTTP correctness from answer usefulness.
Dataset hygiene Keep development examples apart from release holdout.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

C4 evaluation pipeline → handwritten cases/rubric → deterministic checks → small human calibration sample → judge experiment. Evaluate before agent complexity.

Pass Work to do Stop condition
1 · Orient Use the assigned book/article focus and one Arabic or English explanation for each unfamiliar topic. Explain the mechanism in your own words.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L05-E1 Use the twelve public seed cases for practice and author thirty development cases. Include answerable, no-answer, multi-evidence, language, and security categories; review labels manually.
L05-E2 Create a separate holdout before tuning; label document/page/spans. Do not call supplied examples unseen; preserve labels when chunk IDs change.
L05-E3 Write 0–2 rubrics for correctness, groundedness, and relevance. Calibrate a model judge against human review and document disagreements.
L05-E4 Define an evaluation run manifest. Record dataset/model/prompt/corpus versions, case count, latency, and cost or usage.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
One combined RAG score Measure retrieval, citations, generation and system behavior separately.
Judge treated as ground truth Compare human/judge disagreement and inspect bias and failure modes.
Threshold invented after results State intended thresholds before comparing candidates; report sample counts and uncertainty.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. What belongs in a holdout?
  2. Why calibrate an AI judge?
  3. What is an actionable eval failure?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 Cases not used to tune prompts, retrieval settings or model selection.
2 A judge can favor style, verbosity or a model family and miss real task failures.
3 A labeled failure tied to a reproducible case and an identifiable component or product requirement.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S3 Authored evaluation data, rubric, and run manifest; A report showing real failures as well as successes Explain what success means and why. Public seed data is not holdout, mocks do not assess model quality, and judges are not the sole source of truth.

Completion criteria

Explain what success means and why. Public seed data is not holdout, mocks do not assess model quality, and judges are not the sole source of truth.

Postpone until justified

  • An elaborate evaluation platform before useful labels
  • Repeated tuning against the final holdout

python scripts/progress.py set L05 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

The tracker checks dependency/evidence fields; it cannot assess engineering skill automatically.

L06 · PostgreSQL, ORM, and AI Application State

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Tier Focused effort Difficulty Priority Required before starting
2 · Reliable LLM Application Basics 12–18 h 2/5 P1 L01

Unlocks: L07, L11

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Move from Mongoose-style ODM experience to SQLAlchemy relations, sessions, transactions, and Alembic. Design document, conversation, and journal ownership and versions. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
SQL transfer Use joins, foreign keys and transactions without a new beginner course.
API prerequisite Pass IDs and caller identity through a typed application layer.
Persistence reasoning Explain how Mongo document ownership maps to relational keys.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

F7 → Arabic mini-RAG PostgreSQL migration lesson → SQLAlchemy sessions/transactions → Alembic → ownership tests. Reuse your SQL knowledge.

2 · Implement Work through the numbered topic outputs below; integrate them into the assessed exercises. The specified behavior is reproducible in your own code.
3 · Verify Run the failure cases, self-check and milestone acceptance contract. Evidence and remaining limitations are recorded.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L06-E1 Design documents, chunks, conversations, messages, and journals with ownership and relational constraints. Produce an ERD and explain keys, nullability, uniqueness, and ownership boundaries.
L06-E2 Write an initial migration and a version-column migration; simulate a failed write. A fresh database migrates successfully; transaction rollback leaves consistent state.
L06-E3 Implement a session lifecycle per request/task. Concurrent tasks do not share one mutable session; cleanup is tested.
L06-E4 Compare a direct SQL query with the ORM equivalent and add pagination. Explain generated joins and index choices without relearning basic SQL.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
AsyncSession shared across requests Use one session per request/task and explicit transaction boundaries.
Unscoped get-by-ID Every user-owned query includes caller ownership or an authorized join.
Migration only works on your laptop Test upgrade from empty DB and from the previous schema snapshot.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. Session versus transaction?
  2. Why migrations instead of create_all in production?
  3. What makes ingestion idempotent?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 A session manages ORM state and work; a transaction defines the atomic database boundary.
2 Changes must be versioned, reviewable and applied predictably to existing data.
3 A stable identity/version key plus constraints and transaction logic prevent duplicate effects.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S3 ERD, migrations, and real-database integration tests; Session/transaction lifecycle note Rebuild the schema from an empty database and prevent partial writes. Explain where transactions begin and end.

Completion criteria

Rebuild the schema from an empty database and prevent partial writes. Explain where transactions begin and end.

Postpone until justified

  • Multiple databases without a requirement
  • Repeating SQL or MongoDB fundamentals already demonstrated

L07 · RAG Document Preparation, Embeddings, and Semantic Search

Why this Level matters

Convert a small textual PDF into traceable chunks with page provenance. Compare a lexical baseline with embeddings appropriate to the application language. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L07-E1 Start with the JSONL corpus, then a permitted 20–50-page text PDF. Preserve PDF page, printed label if available, and offsets before cleaning.
L07-E2 Handle empty, corrupt, and scanned PDFs explicitly. Unsupported inputs have a clear failure reason rather than an empty ready index.
L07-E3 Compare two chunk configurations and version the embedding setup. Save chunker, document, model, and dimension; never mix incompatible embedding spaces.
L07-E4 Implement a keyword baseline and exact vector retrieval. Evaluate representative queries, including cross-language cases if they belong to the product scope.
L07-E5 Reprocess an identical file and an updated version. No duplicate chunks, no partially published version, and a clear deletion contract for L11.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Text without provenance Keep source ID, page, chunk ordinal, version and owner throughout ingestion.
Choosing an English-only embedding blindly Test Arabic/English queries and follow model-card preprocessing.
ANN before a baseline Use exact search to establish correctness and measure before adding an index.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. Why overlap chunks?
  2. Why store an embedding model version?
  3. Can you filter after returning candidates?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 It can preserve boundary context but increases duplicates, cost and retrieval redundancy.
2 Changing models or dimensions invalidates comparisons and may require re-indexing.
3 Ownership must be enforced in retrieval; post-filtering alone can leak data or destroy recall.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S3 Ingestion functions or CLI, corpus manifest, and provenance tests; Lexical and dense retrieval baseline Every chunk maps to its source. Reprocessing is safe, embedding dimensions match storage, and the implementation remains small enough to debug.

Completion criteria

Every chunk maps to its source. Reprocessing is safe, embedding dimensions match storage, and the implementation remains small enough to debug.

Postpone until justified

  • Distributed processing without scale
  • Premature ANN or OCR

python scripts/progress.py set L07 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

The tracker checks dependency/evidence fields; it cannot assess engineering skill automatically.

This is a curated library of alternatives, implementation references and optional depth. Start with the selected path; do not complete the entire list. Free explanations and local exercises cover the Level without buying a new course. Model API calls, hosted labs, optional GPUs and owned books can have separate costs.

Start here

G4/C6 retrieval basics → mini-RAG processing/search lessons → pgvector exact search → a labeled bilingual retrieval set. Keep page provenance before optimizing.

How to choose and stop

Situation Action
Already comfortable from backend work Demonstrate the diagnostic, skim repeated theory and build the Python-specific behavior.
Arabic introduction helps Use it first, then verify English API names and error behavior in the official reference.
Two sources cover the same thing Finish one explanation, attempt the exercise, and use the second only for the unresolved part.
Tutorial differs from your version Check current docs, pin dependencies and record the adjustment in an ADR.
Everything in the completion gate passes Record the evidence and move to an unlocked Level; bonus reading stays optional.

Verification: resource pages and author/publisher indexes were checked on 2026-10-16. Reachability, title/scope evidence and verification limits are recorded in the audit; the inspectable catalog stores each topic assignment. No claim is made that every video was watched end-to-end or that paid enrollment was tested.

L08 · RAG Systems, Citations, and Retrieval Improvement

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Tier Focused effort Difficulty Priority Required before starting
3 · Grounded Retrieval and Context 18–28 h 3/5 P1 L05, L07

Unlocks: L09, L10, L11, L16

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Build retrieval → context → generation with verifiable citations. Diagnose failures and justify hybrid search or reranking through comparison. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
L05 readiness A labeled query set and separate component metrics exist.
L07 readiness Each returned chunk has valid owner/source/page metadata.
Prompt readiness A model adapter returns validated structured fields.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

Measured exact retrieval → cited generation → no-evidence tests → hybrid/reranking experiments only for observed ranking failures.

Pass Work to do Stop condition
1 · Orient Use the assigned book/article focus and one Arabic or English explanation for each unfamiliar topic. Explain the mechanism in your own words.
2 · Implement Work through the numbered topic outputs below; integrate them into the assessed exercises. The specified behavior is reproducible in your own code.
3 · Verify Run the failure cases, self-check and milestone acceptance contract. Evidence and remaining limitations are recorded.

The resource library contains alternatives and optional depth. The effort estimate covers a selected path plus implementation, not watching every listed course. Choose Arabic teaching when it speeds understanding, then consult the English API reference for exact behavior.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L08-E1 Generate an evidence whitelist, validate references, and display server-provided source/page/snippet. Reject invented IDs and review whether cited passages actually support claims.
L08-E2 Measure hit@5 and recall@5 using evidence labels; run generation with oracle context. Distinguish parsing, retrieval, and generation failures.
L08-E3 Compare lexical and dense retrieval; trial one hybrid/RRF or reranker approach if justified. Keep the same dataset and report quality, latency, and cost differences.
L08-E4 Change one chunking or top-k setting at a time. Save fixed settings and negative results rather than presenting only wins.
L08-E5 Test no-answer, contradictory sources, and an existing but irrelevant citation. The system does not invent support; similarity is not interpreted as answer confidence.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
A citation string equals evidence Resolve source IDs, inspect source content and assess support for the actual claim.
Hybrid search always improves Use the same query set and compare recall/ranking/cost; retain a simpler baseline if better.
Reranking the entire corpus Retrieve candidates first; rerank a bounded list and measure the latency increase.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. What does Recall@k tell you?
  2. Why RRF?
  3. What must abstention test?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 How much of the labeled relevant evidence appears among the first k results; define relevance granularity.
2 It combines ranked lists without assuming their numeric scores are calibrated to each other.
3 No evidence, irrelevant evidence and conflicting evidence; the response should expose uncertainty rather than invent support.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S3 Measured RAG MVP and citation contract tests; Before/after report and failure taxonomy Explain five failures using evidence. References both exist and support the answer under review. Report thresholds as your project targets, not official ITI standards.

Completion criteria

Explain five failures using evidence. References both exist and support the answer under review. Report thresholds as your project targets, not official ITI standards.

Postpone until justified

  • Agentic retrieval before a measured baseline
  • Fine-tuning as a parser repair strategy

L09 · Context Engineering, Memory, and Conversations

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Tier Focused effort Difficulty Priority Required before starting
3 · Grounded Retrieval and Context 14–22 h 3/5 P1 L08

Unlocks: L12, L16

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Budget instructions, evidence, selected journals, history, and memory. Manage memory provenance, correction, deletion, and summarization loss. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
RAG readiness Explain which passages were retrieved and which reached the prompt.
State readiness Conversations and journals are owner-scoped and deletable.
Evaluation readiness Have multi-turn cases with expected facts and source evidence.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

Read Anthropic before agent frameworks → inspect a context trace → budget and retrieve journals → add editable memory → test compaction only when needed.

Pass Work to do Stop condition

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L09-E1 Write a context contract with source priorities and a token budget. Show how each input is selected, bounded, and truncated.
L09-E2 Compare focused evidence against longer distractor-heavy context. Use the same tasks and measure effects rather than assuming more context helps.
L09-E3 Add journal CRUD and connect a user-selected entry to source passages. Clearly separate source claims, journal facts, and assistant suggestions.
L09-E4 Store explicit memory items with owner, source, date, edit, and delete. Do not infer permanent personality traits automatically; changes affect active context.
L09-E5 Trial compaction on a long synthetic conversation. Compare required facts before/after and retain source links and covered message ranges; JIT tools arrive in L12.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Append all history forever Allocate a budget and retrieve only relevant state.
Summary treated as canonical truth Link memories to sources and preserve correction/deletion behavior.
Multi-agent complexity for memory alone Prove a single workflow fails before adding another agent.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. Prompt versus context engineering?
  2. What is just-in-time retrieval?
  3. When is compaction acceptable?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 Prompt writing is part of choosing and managing all instructions, tools, evidence, history and state available to the model.
2 Keep lightweight references and fetch detailed information only when the current step needs it.
3 After measuring retained facts, lost constraints and future-task performance with recoverable source state.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S4 Context contract, journal workflow, and editable memory schema; Focused-context and optional compaction experiments Explain what entered context and why. Deletion is respected and stale summaries are not silently treated as facts.

Completion criteria

Explain what entered context and why. Deletion is respected and stale summaries are not silently treated as facts.

Postpone until justified

  • Uneditable automatic memory
  • Sub-agents before a working bounded single-agent design

Record completion

python scripts/progress.py set L09 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

L10 · Concurrency, Streaming, and Long-Running Jobs

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Tier Focused effort Difficulty Priority Required before starting
4 · Production Behavior and Tool Use 16–24 h 3/5 P1 L08

Unlocks: L14, L16

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Separate I/O concurrency from CPU parallelism and avoid blocking the event loop. Handle streaming cancellation, timeouts, and restartable ingestion jobs. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
API readiness Fake slow/error providers exist so tests are reproducible.
Persistence readiness A job has stable IDs and persisted states.
Concurrency basics Explain I/O waiting versus CPU work.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Recommended learning path

F5 event-loop sections → a blocking-route experiment → F6 streaming → cancellation → mini-RAG Celery lessons and durable ingestion.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L10-E1 Run five concurrent fake I/O requests and probe /health while work is pending. Demonstrate a blocking mistake and a suitable correction; async alone does not solve CPU-heavy parsing.
L10-E2 Implement streaming with a final structured metadata event. Test disconnect, partial provider failure, time limits, and cleanup.
L10-E3 Define queued/running/succeeded/failed/cancelled job states and ownership. Replay duplicate messages and restart a worker without duplicating chunks.
L10-E4 Choose one queue if durability is needed and specify acknowledgment/retry/backoff. Test actual crash behavior; BackgroundTasks is not a durable queue and exactly-once is not assumed.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
async around blocking code A sync parser/model call still blocks; isolate it explicitly.
Retry duplicates writes Use stable job identity and transactional effects.
Browser stops but provider continues Propagate cancellation and record abandoned work and usage.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. When is BackgroundTasks insufficient?
  2. What does at-least-once imply?
  3. Why bound concurrency?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 When work must survive a process restart, needs retries/queues or long-running execution.
2 The same task may execute more than once; effects must be idempotent.
3 To prevent pool exhaustion, provider rate-limit storms and unbounded memory usage.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S4 Concurrency experiment, streaming tests, and job lifecycle; Replay and crash-recovery evidence Light endpoints remain responsive during I/O waits. Partial output is not presented as complete success, and failed indexing never replaces a ready version.

Completion criteria

Light endpoints remain responsive during I/O waits. Partial output is not presented as complete success, and failed indexing never replaces a ready version.

Postpone until justified

  • Additional messaging/workflow platforms without a requirement
  • WebSockets solely because token streaming is needed

Why this Level matters

Enforce ownership before retrieval, source viewing, history, memory, and job access. Constrain untrusted content, tools, uploads, and consumption. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
Auth transfer Explain JWT validation and role versus object permission.
RAG readiness Document retrieval and source display are separate access paths.
Job readiness Job IDs and state can be tied to a caller.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Recommended learning path

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L11-E1 Build an A/B access matrix across every resource endpoint, source viewer, cache, and job state. User B cannot retrieve, read, edit, or delete user A data.
L11-E2 Index a passage that tries to change instructions or expose another journal. Authorization remains independent of the model and document instructions.
L11-E3 Set file/page/token/request/tool/retry limits. Oversized requests and abuse have bounded, tested failure behavior.
L11-E4 Delete a document and its derived data, memory links, and caches. Document active deletion and backup retention separately.
L11-E5 Threat-model upload/storage/provider/logs/tools and use synthetic journals. No secret or sensitive journal content appears in default logs or browser configuration.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Prompt as an access-control system Permission checks execute in backend code before data/tools are exposed.
Sanitize then trust everything Treat retrieved text/tool output as untrusted throughout execution.
Log all prompts for debugging Redact sensitive fields and define retention/erasure behavior.

Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 Identity verification versus permission to perform a specific action on a specific object.
2 A document or tool result can carry instructions even when the user query is benign.
3 Separate cited book content, personal journal facts and generated suggestions; avoid unsupported clinical claims.

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Distinguish fixed workflows from model-directed tool loops. Implement safe tool contracts and evaluate tool/task failures. Understand sub-agent patterns without assuming they improve the product. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L12-E1 Define read-only search_passages and get_passage tools with bounded outputs. Validate schemas and enforce authenticated ownership; no generic SQL or shell tool.
L12-E2 Build a small direct-SDK loop with max steps, timeout, and token budget. Handle unknown tool, invalid arguments, no-tool-needed, and repeated-loop cases.

L12-E4 Use lightweight references and fetch passages just in time. Only permitted requested content is loaded; compare against upfront retrieval.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Every problem needs an agent Compare with deterministic routing/workflows first.
Tool execution trusts model arguments Validate schema and permission for every call, independent of model compliance.
Unbounded self-correction Enforce steps, wall time and token/cost budgets in code.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. Workflow versus agent?
  2. What is a trajectory failure?
  3. When add sub-agents?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 The workflow fixes control flow in code; the agent chooses next actions within enforced limits.
2 An invalid/unnecessary/repeated tool sequence even if the final prose looks plausible.
3 A separable task and a measured improvement justify coordination, isolation and extra cost.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S5 Tool contracts, bounded agent loop, and evaluation report; Single-agent versus multi-agent ADR A model request is never treated as authorization. Loops stop predictably and you can justify either using an agent or retaining a fixed workflow.

Completion criteria

A model request is never treated as authorization. Loops stop predictably and you can justify either using an agent or retaining a fixed workflow.

Postpone until justified

  • Framework orchestration before understanding a loop
  • Multi-agent implementation just to check a syllabus keyword

Record completion

Run from the roadmap root:

python scripts/progress.py set L12 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

The tracker checks dependency/evidence fields; it cannot assess engineering skill automatically.

L13 · MCP, Enterprise Integration, and AI Workflows

4 · Production Behavior and Tool Use 10–16 h 3/5 P2 L12

Unlocks: L16

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Understand MCP host/client/server roles, primitives, transport, and authorization. Build one bounded integration with explicit failure behavior. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
Tool prerequisites A typed read-only operation already works without MCP.
Security prerequisites Caller identity and tool/data permissions are explicit.
Protocol reasoning Distinguish app orchestration, transport and model behavior.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

Official MCP architecture + Arabic translated index → Python SDK server/client → permission boundary → failure/reconnect tests. Use a single local integration first.

Pass Work to do Stop condition
1 · Orient Use the assigned book/article focus and one Arabic or English explanation for each unfamiliar topic. Explain the mechanism in your own words.
2 · Implement Work through the numbered topic outputs below; integrate them into the assessed exercises. The specified behavior is reproducible in your own code.
3 · Verify Run the failure cases, self-check and milestone acceptance contract. Evidence and remaining limitations are recorded.

The resource library contains alternatives and optional depth. The effort estimate covers a selected path plus implementation, not watching every listed course. Choose Arabic teaching when it speeds understanding, then consult the English API reference for exact behavior.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L13-E1 Contrast function calling with an MCP integration diagram. Identify who manages context and authorization; neither is delegated to the protocol.
L13-E2 Expose get_passage through a local MCP server and call it from one client. Pin the protocol/SDK version and record successful discovery and invocation.
L13-E3 Test malformed arguments, unauthorized documents, and timeout. Connection failure does not widen permissions.
L13-E4 Design a reading-summary draft workflow with one simulated save operation. The application enforces approval, authorization, and duplicate handling for writes; no external message needs to be sent.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
MCP grants permission automatically Protocol interoperability is separate from authorization.
SDK example without version pin Pin SDK/protocol and document transport compatibility.
Remote server output becomes trusted instructions Validate and scope results; keep business decisions in application code.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. Host/client/server?
  2. Tools versus resources?
  3. Why not add MCP everywhere?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 The host application manages model interaction, the client maintains a server connection, and the server exposes capabilities.
2 Tools perform callable operations; resources expose addressable context. Neither bypasses permissions.
3 Use it for interoperable external integrations; direct internal calls can be simpler.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S5 Small MCP server/client demo and integration diagram; Version record and permission/timeout tests Demonstrate one real tool invocation through a client and explain where permissions, context, approval, and retries live.

Completion criteria

Demonstrate one real tool invocation through a client and explain where permissions, context, approval, and retries live.

Postpone until justified

  • Many MCP servers or invented enterprise integrations
  • Production remote authentication before a local protocol demonstration

python scripts/progress.py set L13 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"
