Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L14-E1 Document application architecture and ADRs for inference, state, and worker boundaries. Explain failure boundaries and why a modular monolith is sufficient.
L14-E2 Add trace IDs and stage timings for parsing/retrieval/model calls, TTFT, errors, and usage. Sensitive content is redacted; representative failures can be located from a trace.
L14-E3 Build CI for deterministic tests and checks, with separately budgeted live evaluation. Model or prompt changes trigger an explicit regression review.
L14-E4 Containerize and test fresh migration, persistent restart, backup/restore, and readiness. Record an actual restore, not just a backup command.
L14-E5 Deploy once to a suitable environment or demonstrate a reproducible local operating setup. Test outage and rollback; public access requires HTTPS/auth/secrets controls.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Container runs therefore production-ready Test persistence, health, secrets, worker restart and restore.
Only log final model text Trace parser/retriever/provider stages while redacting private content.
Deploy with no recovery plan Test restore/rollback and define who can apply a migration.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. What belongs in a trace?
  2. What must be versioned?
  3. Backup equals restore?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 A correlation ID, stage timing, selected model/config and safe error/usage attributes; avoid raw private journals.
2 Code, model, prompt, corpus/index, embedding/chunk policy and evaluation set.
3 No; a backup is evidence only after a successful restore and consistency check.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S6 Runbook, CI, release manifest, and traces; Deployment/local operating demo plus restore evidence Reproduce a release, restore state, diagnose failure, and state operating limits. Docker alone is not production readiness.

Completion criteria

Reproduce a release, restore state, diagnose failure, and state operating limits. Docker alone is not production readiness.

Postpone until justified

  • Kubernetes or multiple observability products by default
  • A GPU cluster before a measured serving requirement

Run from the roadmap root:

python scripts/progress.py set L14 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

The tracker checks dependency/evidence fields; it cannot assess engineering skill automatically.

How to choose and stop

Situation Action
Already comfortable from backend work Demonstrate the diagnostic, skim repeated theory and build the Python-specific behavior.
Arabic introduction helps Use it first, then verify English API names and error behavior in the official reference.
Two sources cover the same thing Finish one explanation, attempt the exercise, and use the second only for the unresolved part.
Tutorial differs from your version Check current docs, pin dependencies and record the adjustment in an ADR.
Everything in the completion gate passes Record the evidence and move to an unlocked Level; bonus reading stays optional.

Reachability, title/scope evidence and verification limits are recorded in the audit; the inspectable catalog stores each topic assignment. No claim is made that every video was watched end-to-end or that paid enrollment was tested.

L15 · Model Selection, Cost, Latency, and User Feedback

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Tier Focused effort Difficulty Priority Required before starting
5 · Operating and Demonstrating AI Systems 12–18 h 4/5 P2 L05, L14

Unlocks: L16, L17

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Improve a measured bottleneck without sacrificing quality or isolation. Interpret user feedback as a signal rather than unquestioned labels. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
Baseline readiness Quality, citation, security and latency results have version metadata.
Observability readiness Measure stage timing and usage without private content logging.
Selection discipline Hold corpus/prompt/eval fixed for model comparison.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

C4 model selection → comparable runs → C9 metrics → one bottleneck experiment → C10 feedback loop. Keep quality gates active during optimization.

Pass Work to do Stop condition
1 · Orient Use the assigned book/article focus and one Arabic or English explanation for each unfamiliar topic. Explain the mechanism in your own words.
2 · Implement Work through the numbered topic outputs below; integrate them into the assessed exercises. The specified behavior is reproducible in your own code.
3 · Verify Run the failure cases, self-check and milestone acceptance contract. Evidence and remaining limitations are recorded.

The resource library contains alternatives and optional depth. The effort estimate covers a selected path plus implementation, not watching every listed course. Choose Arabic teaching when it speeds understanding, then consult the English API reference for exact behavior.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L15-E1 Compare two model settings or models on the same workload. Report case count, quality, usage/cost basis, p50/p95, TTFT, date, and environment.
L15-E2 Change context size, output length, or retrieval calls as one controlled experiment. A latency win that breaks citation quality is not presented as an overall improvement.
L15-E3 If caching is justified, define tenant/model/prompt/corpus keys and invalidation. Test cross-user isolation and deletion before enabling the cache.
L15-E4 Classify synthetic thumbs/comments into style, factual, retrieval, and other failures. Define a review loop; dislike is not automatically a factual-error training label.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Leaderboard wins equal product wins Evaluate your bilingual corpus and workload under actual constraints.
Global cache key Include ownership, model/prompt/corpus versions and deletion invalidation.
Thumbs-up equals ground truth Feedback is biased and sparse; review failure examples before changing behavior.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. Why p95 as well as p50?
  2. Cost per request?
  3. When optimize?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 Median hides slow tail experiences; report concurrency and sample count too.
2 Measure input/output tokens and provider prices or local compute assumptions, plus retries/reranking when relevant.
3 After a reproducible bottleneck is identified and a quality/permission regression gate exists.

Capstone checkpoint

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S6 Model scorecard, controlled optimization report, and feedback taxonomy Every decision has a baseline, configuration, sample size, and limitations. Similarity is not answer confidence and feedback is not automatically ground truth.

Completion criteria

Every decision has a baseline, configuration, sample size, and limitations. Similarity is not answer confidence and feedback is not automatically ground truth.

Postpone until justified

  • Semantic caching before invalidation rules
  • Many-model routing or GPU tuning without a bottleneck

How to choose and stop

Situation Action
Already comfortable from backend work Demonstrate the diagnostic, skim repeated theory and build the Python-specific behavior.
Arabic introduction helps Use it first, then verify English API names and error behavior in the official reference.
Two sources cover the same thing Finish one explanation, attempt the exercise, and use the second only for the unresolved part.
Tutorial differs from your version Check current docs, pin dependencies and record the adjustment in an ADR.
Everything in the completion gate passes Record the evidence and move to an unlocked Level; bonus reading stays optional.

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Demonstrate a usable AI application with evaluation and operating evidence. Explain and modify the implementation independently and communicate honest limitations. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
Core project readiness Upload, ask, cite, journal connection, history and deletion work end-to-end.
Evidence readiness CI, holdout, security matrix and traces have reproducible artifacts.
Independence Modify and test a small feature without generated code.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

No new large course: assemble the evidence, run an untouched holdout, ship a reproducible release and defend the decisions. Apply while improving documented gaps.

Pass Work to do Stop condition
1 · Orient Use the assigned book/article focus and one Arabic or English explanation for each unfamiliar topic. Explain the mechanism in your own words.
2 · Implement Work through the numbered topic outputs below; integrate them into the assessed exercises. The specified behavior is reproducible in your own code.
3 · Verify Run the failure cases, self-check and milestone acceptance contract. Evidence and remaining limitations are recorded.

The resource library contains alternatives and optional depth. The effort estimate covers a selected path plus implementation, not watching every listed course. Choose Arabic teaching when it speeds understanding, then consult the English API reference for exact behavior.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L16-E1 Demonstrate upload → question → source → journal connection → conversation → deletion through a basic UI. Include failure paths and cross-user access tests.
L16-E2 Freeze configuration and run the final independent holdout. Analyze three major failures and disclose remaining limitations.
L16-E3 Publish application setup docs, ERD/architecture, ADRs, runbook, and a 3–5-minute demo. A reviewer can reproduce the demo with synthetic data.
L16-E4 Run a mock defense: write an endpoint, query, and test without AI; diagnose a trace. Explain framework, model, retrieval, and single-agent decisions.
L16-E5 Draft evidence-based CV claims and a scoped freelance proposal. Only measured figures are used; discuss data ethics, delivery scope, and communication.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Another demo without evidence Link reproducible tests, datasets, configs and failure analysis.
Production claim exceeds deployment Describe what was exercised and remaining limits precisely.
Study every optional resource before applying Use the role checklist and apply after demonstrable core competence.

Question Expected reasoning

L17 · Fine-Tuning, Dataset Engineering, and Advanced Serving

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Choose between prompt changes, RAG, model selection, and fine-tuning using failure evidence. Understand SFT/LoRA, curation, quantization, and serving tradeoffs. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
Failure evidence Show a task failure that prompting, retrieval or model choice did not solve.
Training readiness Explain loss, optimizer, leakage and held-out evaluation.
Budget readiness Choose hardware/runtime and stop conditions before any training run.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Optional: C7 go/no-go → curated train/dev/holdout data → one small LoRA experiment → compare to baseline → serve only if justified.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L17-E1 Write a decision memo contrasting missing knowledge with task/style adaptation. Choose not to fine-tune when the failure evidence does not justify it.
L17-E2 Design a training dataset card with schema, rights, privacy, deduplication, and splits. Private journals are not implicitly repurposed as training data.
L17-E3 Optionally run a tiny PEFT experiment on permitted data if resources allow. Compare a prompt baseline on independent holdout; otherwise deliver a resource-aware experiment plan.
L17-E4 Optionally compare quantized or batched local serving. Report memory/latency/quality on the actual hardware; no invented runs.

Use the evidence template for code/tests and the experiment template for comparisons. The external lab choices are in Practice and quizzes.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Fine-tuning for changing factual documents RAG may be a better mechanism; demonstrate the actual adaptation need.
Train/eval near-duplicates Split by source or task group and audit leakage.
Quantization improves everything Measure memory, speed and quality; hardware compatibility varies.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. When fine-tune?
  2. What is LoRA?
  3. What must compare?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 When a stable task/behavior gap and sufficient representative data justify training and maintenance costs.
2 A parameter-efficient adaptation method using trainable low-rank updates instead of full-weight training.
3 The same independent task cases before/after tuning, including regressions, memory, latency and cost.

Milestone Artifacts Acceptance contract
S7 Customization decision, dataset card, and experiment plan; Run report only if a training/serving experiment was actually executed A conceptual decision can be completed without a GPU. Practical training is not marked done without run evidence. This module is optional for Core completion.

Completion criteria

A conceptual decision can be completed without a GPU. Practical training is not marked done without run evidence. This module is optional for Core completion.

Postpone until justified

  • Training a foundation model
  • Paying for compute without a hypothesis and evaluation

Record completion

Run from the roadmap root:

python scripts/progress.py set L17 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

The tracker checks dependency/evidence fields; it cannot assess engineering skill automatically.

L18 · Supporting TypeScript and React UI

← Roadmap · Resource library · ITI coverage · Progress

Tier Focused effort Difficulty Priority Required before starting
6 · Optional Specialization and Supporting UI 12–20 h 2/5 P3 · optional L01, L04, L11

Unlocks: No further required Level; use the evidence to apply or specialize.

Start path · Topics · Books · Practice · Completion gate

Why this Level matters

Build a small accessible client for API errors, streaming, and evidence display. Distinguish product UI needs from a complete React/TypeScript curriculum. The output contributes to the same Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant; build the implementation yourself in a separate application repository.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

Check Demonstrate before starting
JavaScript transfer Use promises, modules and HTTP requests without a full JS course.
Backend readiness Stable auth, upload, question and source endpoints exist.
Product contract Define streaming events and the citation response schema.

Use existing CRM work as evidence where applicable. If a check fails, use the targeted remedial resource; a full beginner course is unnecessary when you can demonstrate the skill.

Learning objectives

Recommended learning path

Optional supporting UI: Elzero TypeScript gaps → React official quick path → fetch/abort → source display and browser tests. A minimal client is sufficient.

Pass Work to do Stop condition
1 · Orient Use the assigned book/article focus and one Arabic or English explanation for each unfamiliar topic. Explain the mechanism in your own words.
2 · Implement Work through the numbered topic outputs below; integrate them into the assessed exercises. The specified behavior is reproducible in your own code.
3 · Verify Run the failure cases, self-check and milestone acceptance contract. Evidence and remaining limitations are recorded.

The resource library contains alternatives and optional depth. The effort estimate covers a selected path plus implementation, not watching every listed course. Choose Arabic teaching when it speeds understanding, then consult the English API reference for exact behavior.

Practical exercises

The topic outputs are small checkpoints that feed these assessed exercises. Combine them where appropriate rather than creating a separate project for each row.

ID Implement or investigate Acceptance evidence
L18-E1 Type requests, responses, and citations; add upload/question/journal forms. Handle loading, empty, and failure states.

Common mistakes and debugging guidance

Failure pattern Correction
Full frontend specialization Implement only the capstone user flows.
Client guards replace backend auth Server ownership tests remain mandatory.
Render model output as arbitrary HTML Use safe rendering and explicit source links; avoid unsanitized HTML.

Self-check before moving on

Answer without looking at the resources, then inspect the guide below. These are original practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

  1. Why a discriminated union for API results?
  2. Why AbortController?
  3. What proves usable citations?
Answer guide and reasoning checks
Question Expected reasoning
1 It forces success/error/stream states to be handled explicitly.
2 It cancels a fetch/stream and lets the UI stop unnecessary work.
3 Keyboard-accessible source links resolve to authorized passages with page/context and clear loading/error states.

Evaluation and Evidence Standards

Home · L05 · Experiment template

Primary references: C3–4, C6, and F11. Evaluate components separately; HTTP200 does not establish answer quality. The following are project recommendations, not official ITI thresholds.

Dataset rule Application
Public seed Twelve supplied synthetic cases are practice data, never unseen holdout
Development Author approximately 30 reviewed cases covering normal and failure behavior
Final holdout Keep approximately 20 additional cases independent of tuning
Labels Query, answerability, expected facts, document/page/span evidence, category, and language
Human review Do not accept model-generated labels or answers as gold without review
Changes Preserve source-level labels when chunk IDs change
Component Measure Important distinction
Parsing Text/page correctness and explicit failure reasons Empty or scanned PDF is not a successful empty index
Retrieval Hit@k and recall@k Any required evidence versus the fraction of all required evidence retrieved
Generation Correctness, groundedness, and relevance on 0–2 rubrics Use oracle context to isolate generation failure
Citations Valid IDs and actual claim support An existing ID may still support nothing in the answer
Abstention Appropriate no-answer and false-refusal rates Refusing every query is not success
Agents Task success, tool/argument errors, limit violations, and unauthorized attempts Evaluate actions and outcomes, not just final prose
Context Required facts retained, stale memory, and distractor effects Longer context is not automatically better
Serving p50/p95, TTFT, error rate, usage, and cost basis Report workload, environment, sample count, and date
Check type Dependencies What it establishes
Unit/API Fake providers Contracts, branches, failures, and validation
Integration Real test database/retriever Ownership, persistence, metadata, and component boundaries
Live evaluation Actual model with a separate budget Model/application behavior under versioned inputs
Model judge Calibrated against human review Helpful imperfect scoring, not sole ground truth

Keep a baseline and change one variable per experiment. Negative findings are valid evidence. Small latency samples are descriptive, not a general reliability benchmark. Record the source/date of prices rather than inventing current costs.

Context Engineering

Home · L09 · L12

Anthropic’s article motivates deliberate context selection and maintenance, retrieval when needed, and strategies for long-running work. The applications below are proposed project exercises, supported by C5 context efficiency and C6 memory; they are not mandatory steps from the article.

Concept Module Project exercise
Prompt versus context L04/L09 Separate instructions from evidence, history, journals, and memory
Context rot L09 Compare focused context against distracting material
Selection L08/L09 Budget and prioritize retrieved evidence and recent turns
Just-in-time retrieval L12 Load a permitted passage through a bounded tool
Tool design L12 Clear schemas, concise results, and useful errors
Compaction L09 Test retention of required facts before/after summarization
Structured memory L09 Owner/source/date with correction and deletion
Sub-agents L12 Optional comparison using isolated context and a summary contract
Product boundary Requirement
Journals The user chooses what enters context
Memory Editable, sourced, dated, and scoped; avoid automatic permanent personality conclusions
Summaries Preserve covered message ranges and evidence provenance; stale summaries may be wrong
Authorization The server authorizes access before loading content into context
Testing Measure lost facts, stale values, contradictory entries, and deletion behavior

Security and Responsible AI

Home · L11 · Threat model

References: F8–9, C5, and OWASP GenAI. Documents, journals, and tool outputs are untrusted inputs. RAG or fine-tuning does not automatically eliminate prompt injection.

Boundary Required behavior Evidence
Identity Derive the user on the server, never from model-chosen authority A/B access tests
Retrieval Filter ownership before context construction Cross-user search and source-viewer tests
Tools Least privilege and server-side argument/permission checks Unknown/unauthorized tool tests
Writes Appropriate approval and idempotency Simulated repeated-write/failure tests
Consumption Bound upload/pages/tokens/requests/steps/retries Oversized and abuse cases
Secrets Keep keys out of repository, browser, prompts, and logs Configuration/log review
Journals Do not log sensitive contents by default Redaction and retention review
Deletion Remove active derived content and document backup retention Delete/re-query/context/cache tests
Deployment HTTPS, appropriate CORS/cookie/bearer/CSRF choices Public-demo gate
Before public access Status to record
API/source/stream/job authorization Tested with multiple users
Provider outage and partial stream failure Tested
Persistence and restore Demonstrated
Demo inputs and budgets Synthetic and bounded
Residual risk Documented; passing designed tests is not universal proof of security

Effort and Pacing

Home · Progress

The comprehensive Core is approximately 233–355 focused hours before diagnostic waivers. These are personal learning estimates, not ITI hours or an obligation to finish everything within twelve weeks.

Availability Adaptation
15–20 hours/week Approximately12–24 weeks for Core; S3 produces a demonstrable RAG application earlier
8–10 hours/week Extend the calendar while retaining prerequisites and security/evaluation gates
25+ hours/week Spend extra time on failure analysis and review rather than adding frameworks
Example18-hour allocation 4 reading,9 building,3 tests/evaluation/docs,2 foundations or review; adjust as needed
Situation Action
Familiar backend topic Pass a diagnostic and a small Python transfer exercise; skim repeated theory
Whole-module waiver Demonstrate every gate, record evidence and reason, and keep it visible as waived
Stuck implementation Record a blocker, reduce to one behavior, and read to answer a specific question
Independent foundations Progress L02 alongside implementation; do not silently remove it from Core
Early job application Use actual milestones and existing CRM evidence; do not wait for every optional topic

Completing one familiar subtopic does not waive an entire module. ORM lifecycle, model evaluation, and LLM security may remain new even with substantial backend experience.

Progress Tracker

Home · GitHub progress tables · Tracker CSV · Evidence

All 19 modules and8 milestones start at not_started. Core and optional progress are reported separately; waivers are visible rather than silently counted as completed study.

Action Command
View status python scripts/progress.py status
Refresh GitHub tables python scripts/progress.py export
Find eligible work python scripts/progress.py next
Start a module python scripts/progress.py set L00 in_progress
Record completion python scripts/progress.py set L00 completed --evidence "progress/evidence/m00.md"
Record an earned waiver python scripts/progress.py set L01 waived --evidence "your-test-report" --reason "passed every gate independently"
Reopen work python scripts/progress.py set L00 not_started
Status Rule
not_started No completion claimed
in_progress Hard prerequisites must be accepted
completed Prerequisites and an evidence field are required
waived Prerequisites, evidence, and a diagnostic reason are required
blocked A reason is required; use it to document a real blocker

Run commands from the repository root. Reopening a prerequisite is rejected while an active descendant depends on it; reopen descendants first. Manual CSV edits can be checked with validate.py. Tool-generated timestamps use UTC.

The set command refreshes PROGRESS.md automatically for the default tracker. After manual CSV edits, run export. The HTML overview is a build-time snapshot; rebuild it if you want its progress tables refreshed.

The CLI checks sequence and fields, not skill quality or whether a report proves its claims. Review evidence yourself. L17/L18 do not block Core completion. No learning outcomes have been completed on your behalf.

Extract the ZIP and open the folder in VS Code or browse the Markdown on GitHub. Start with README.md, then START_HERE.md. The standalone OVERVIEW.html requires no installation.

Put it on GitHub

Step Action
1 Create an empty GitHub repository under your account
2 Open a terminal inside the extracted repository and run git init
3 Review files and run the validation commands
4 Commit the roadmap and push to the remote you created
5 Keep application code in a separate implementation repository and link evidence here

The roadmap is maintained in the existing private GitHub repository; this ZIP is a complete portable copy. The ZIP excludes caches, secrets, and book PDFs. Do not commit personal journals or copyright-protected book text. Libraries for application/ML exercises are installed only when needed, in separate environments.

Updated resource edition

All Level content is maintained in its numbered directory. Use the resource policy/audit and the two authoring templates when adding material. docs/RESOURCE_CATALOG.json records topic assignments and RESOURCE_VERIFICATION.json stores the dated URL check. Publishing is handled separately from curriculum validation; repository visibility remains private.

Interviews and Job Readiness

Home · L16 · Portfolio checklist

The Native AI brochure lists programming/DSA, SQL, Git/CLI, conceptual mathematics, and technical English as preparation. Questions below are inferred practice prompts, not confirmed ITI interview questions.

Area Practice task
Python/FastAPI Write a typed endpoint and fixture; explain blocking I/O and cleanup
Mathematics/ML/DL Calculate cosine and precision/recall; explain leakage, autograd, and inference versus training
RAG Isolate retrieval failure from generation failure; distinguish reference validity from supporting evidence
Context Decide what to truncate and how to correct/delete stale memory
Agents/MCP Justify an agent over a workflow and locate authorization and protocol boundaries
LLMOps Diagnose a trace, demonstrate rollback/recovery, and explain evaluation regression and cost
Target role Additional portfolio evidence
Junior AI Application Engineer LLM integration, typed outputs, RAG, evaluation, isolation, and deployment
Junior LLM/RAG Engineer Parsing/retrieval comparisons, evidence labels, citation review, and failure analysis
AI Backend Developer Python/ORM/transactions, async, jobs, model-provider boundaries, and operational tests

Role titles vary; compare each actual job description. This roadmap contains no hiring statistics or acceptance guarantee. Use measured figures for CV claims and retain the underlying report. Existing CRM remains backend evidence; optional fine-tuning or advanced React need not delay relevant applications.

Learning with AI Assistance

Home

Stage Your responsibility
Attempt Work for25–40 minutes and describe expected behavior, observations, and errors
Ask Request a hint, explanation, or focused review rather than an entire implementation
Implement Write, run, and test the solution yourself
Verify understanding Explain the code and make a small requirement change without copying
Review labels Human-review evaluation labels and important decisions
Protect data Understand what is sent to a service; never include secrets or private journals casually
Record evidence Identify what you built, what failed, why you chose an approach, and how to reproduce it

A working solution you cannot explain is still a learning gap. AI may review a test; it does not turn unreviewed model output into ground truth.

Resource selection and verification policy

Roadmap · Level audit · Book locator

How the library is curated

Decision Rule
Topic first A resource must teach a named topic, supply a relevant implementation reference, or support a specific exercise. A channel homepage is not used as a substitute for a lesson.
One practical route The owned book/article, one explanation and the actual project exercise form the path. Extra courses are alternatives, not cumulative prerequisites.
Arabic teaching Identify the instructor and specific lesson where available. Egyptian mini-RAG, Python, ML and TypeScript teaching is distinguished from translated text.
Arabic translations Microsoft translations identify Co-op Translator. They are labelled machine-translated text; the linked English video is not called Arabic instruction.
Partial coverage When an Arabic lesson supplies only prerequisites or orientation, the topic row states that boundary. A missing exact resource is recorded as a gap.
English teaching Prefer course/lecture pages containing real videos, notebooks or exercises. Current official docs resolve API/version differences.
Paid alternatives Recommend only a clearly relevant alternative with an explicit reason to buy. No new paid course is necessary for completion.
Practice Distinguish self-assessed local tasks from platform quizzes, automated checks and hosted labs. A concept quiz does not assess a working system.
Advanced material Papers and deeper serving/agent patterns follow demonstrated need. They do not block the first working application.

What was verified

The discovery process inspected author/publisher course outlines, instructor lesson indexes, official documentation pages and relevant content excerpts. For mini-RAG, the instructor’s tut-017 README lists direct video titles and matching code branches; the older main README stops earlier. Hesham Asem’s own course index identifies the ML playlists. Elzero’s study plans identify lesson subjects and assignments. DeepLearning.AI pages show lesson outlines and access terms. Microsoft Arabic lesson pages were opened after correcting their translation paths.

The final HTTP audit issues GET requests, follows redirects and records status, final URL, page title and check date. The machine-readable evidence is in RESOURCE_VERIFICATION.json. This verifies reachability at a point in time; a 200 response alone does not prove instructional quality. Topic assignment was separately reviewed against the source’s title, outline or documentation scope.

Verification limits

  • Not every video was watched end-to-end. Arabic video topic identity is supported by instructor-authored lesson/course indexes and reachable lesson URLs; playable media can still vary by account, region or later removal.
  • Paid enrollments and account-only assessments were not purchased or completed. Regional prices are not invented. Auto-generated Arabic subtitles do not make an English course an Arabic course.
  • DeepLearning.AI advertises limited-time free course access and separately promotes Pro accomplishments. Access and assessment terms can change; the official/local alternative remains available.
  • Historical courses can teach valid architecture while showing obsolete dependencies. Specific cautions are retained in the resource tables.
  • Book assignments use the three supplied PDFs’ verified contents and page conventions. Books are referenced, not redistributed. No additional chapter numbers were guessed.
  • These resources and practice questions are recommendations. They are not an official ITI reading list or confirmed interview questions.

Recheck after editing

python scripts/audit_level_content.py
python scripts/audit_resources.py
python scripts/validate.py

The content audit runs offline. The resource audit needs network access and reports failures rather than silently replacing a URL or treating a login wall as proof of full access. Review failures before changing a curated assignment.

Level Content and Resource Audit

Roadmap · Selection policy · Reference comparison

Audit date: 16 October 2026. The audit covers all 19 Levels, including optional fine-tuning and the supporting UI. The AI Engineering scope and the original ITI mapping remain unchanged.

Check Result
Level pairs 19 READMEs + 19 resource libraries rewritten with the same section contract.
Topic mapping 113 important topic groups have subtopics, Arabic choice/gap, English route, book/article focus, implementation reference and practical output.
Resource catalog 153 distinct assigned resources. Each has provider, language, format, access terms, purpose and scope limitation.
Reachability 153/153 catalog URLs returned HTTP 200 in the final GET audit; redirects/titles are retained in RESOURCE_VERIFICATION.json.
Arabic labels Egyptian/Arabic videos are separated from Microsoft machine-translated Arabic text. Partial matches and exact-topic gaps are disclosed.
Books Existing supplied-PDF chapter/page assignments retained; no new unverified chapter numbers and no book redistribution.
External assessment Platform quizzes/labs are labelled separately from the Level’s self-assessed drills and original questions.
Prerequisites and projects Existing dependency graph, 84 assessed exercises, eight milestones and evidence tracker retained.

Reproducible checks

python scripts/audit_level_content.py
python scripts/validate.py
python -m unittest discover -s tests -v
## Requires network; writes a fresh dated status report:
python scripts/audit_resources.py

The offline audit checks required sections, topic fields, mapped IDs, minimum resource variety, and every Markdown file/directory/heading-fragment target. It cannot judge pedagogy or learner competence. Source relevance was reviewed separately against the publisher/instructor material; full video playback, paid access and every hosted assessment were not tested.

Reference-Level Comparison and Revision

Roadmap · Audit

Structural reference: Yahia Sherif’s Data Engineering Roadmap 2026, inspected at commit 4ba9d36fbfc809e7ebb087e651e1df86aea6f2bc. The following are comparable engineering roles in the curriculum, not claims that AI and Data Engineering subjects are interchangeable. No Data Engineering specialization, advertised hours or level count was imported.

Representative purpose Reference README + resources AI README + resources Finding and implemented change
Foundation/API transfer 04-Python 01-Python-FastAPI Reference connects topics to Arabic and English teaching and gives a categorized resource library. AI L01 previously had four documentation links. It now distinguishes Python remediation, API validation, lifecycle, testing and their matching lessons/references.
Production quality/operations 23-Governance-Quality-Observability 14-LLMOps-Deployment Reference separates teaching, practice, documentation and optional depth and admits Arabic scarcity. AI L14 previously reused Docker links for recovery. It now maps deployment, CI, versions, telemetry and recovery to distinct resources and an actual restore drill.
Capstone/evidence 26-Capstone 16-Capstone-Career Reference treats capstone as an evidence-producing system. AI L16 previously had four broad references. It now supplies release, evaluation, defense, role-specific communication and independent implementation guidance with matched learning resources.
